# Ralph Loop Progress — Headball Multi-Game Platform

Branch: feat/multigame-platform
Total stories: 83
Started: 2026-05-08

## Codebase Patterns

- **Server-side time-guard E2E pattern (per US-069 / mark_correct_guess late rejection):** to verify "RPC X rejects after deadline" without waiting time_limit_s seconds (and without changing the validated time-limit range), (1) drive the room into the phase that exposes the guarded action, (2) service-role `update game_insider_round set started_at = now() - 10min` AFTER the page that owns the action has already mounted (its props are cached at mount), (3) click the UI control normally — the server reconciles + raises the deadline-specific errcode, the transaction rolls back per pattern note 40, (4) assert UI shows the mapped error message AND DB phase is unchanged (proves rollback), (5) explicitly fire the public reconcile RPC (expire_round / equivalent) to commit the deferred phase flip, (6) assert UI re-routes to the post-deadline screen. The `master-mark-correct-cta` button is intentionally NOT gated on `remainingS<=0` (only the response yes/no/unsure buttons are) so this UI path actually exercises the server guard rather than dying at a client-side disabled gate. Reusable shape for future late-action specs (PGAME19 cast_vote-after-vote-deadline, etc.).
- **`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` is required for concurrent-safe scoring RPCs (per US-062 / migration 0032):** US-049's `advance_to_reveal` (migration 0028) used `scored_at` as the idempotency stamp but the read-and-act pattern was race-prone — when N reveal-phase clients all fire the RPC simultaneously per T-3.B "anyone advances", every concurrent call read `scored_at IS NULL` BEFORE any of them committed, then all proceeded to apply the +2 score deltas to `players.total_score`, multiplying the score N times. Fix migration 0032 added `for update` to the initial round-row read so PostgreSQL serializes the concurrent calls; the first call applies scoring + stamps `scored_at`, subsequent calls block until commit then early-return via the existing `if v_scored_at is not null then return` guard. **Reusable rule:** any RPC that uses a "stamp column" (e.g. `scored_at`, `applied_at`, `consumed_at`) for idempotency AND performs cumulative side effects (incrementing counters, awarding scores, decrementing stock) MUST `select ... for update` the row that holds the stamp column. The trailing `update where stamp is null` only protects the stamp itself; the side effects above it still race. The WHERE-clause idempotency pattern (used in `advance_to_voting`, `advance_to_asking`, `cast_vote`) is fine for pure phase-flip RPCs because the only mutation IS the conditional UPDATE — but the moment a function does additional cumulative writes, you need the row lock.
- **Reveal-phase secret exposure goes through a public RPC, not a column-grant flip (per US-062 / migration 0033):** the asymmetric-secret column-RLS pattern from US-038 (`game_insider_round.secret_value` denied to anon, fetched by Master/Insider via SECURITY DEFINER `get_my_insider_secret`) extends to the reveal phase via a SECOND public RPC `get_revealed_secret(p_room_id, p_round, p_player_id)`. Membership-check (PGAME11) + phase guard (`phase in ('reveal', 'result_failed')`, else PGAME21) gates the read; PGAME22 = "round not found". Don't add `secret_value` to the anon column grant when phase advances to reveal — that would leak it during 'asking' too (column grants are not phase-aware). And don't write a Realtime broadcast carve-out (column-list publication can't be edited in place). The two-RPC pattern (Master/Insider during play, all-players at reveal) is the canonical shape for any future asymmetric-secret game with a public-reveal phase. The reveal screen's initial-fetch effect calls `getRevealedSecret` (catching errors silently) instead of selecting from `game_insider_round` directly; everything else (players, roles, votes — all anon-readable per migration 0019/0018) selects normally.
- **`advance_to_next_round` returns to LOBBY by design (per US-062 / migration 0031):** the C2.A state-machine "reveal → next round" transition for Phase 5b.7a is implemented as: T-3.B "anyone advances" RPC (`advance_to_next_round(p_room_id, p_round, p_player_id)`) flips `rooms.status` from PLAYING → LOBBY, preserving `players` rows + `players.total_score` + the just-completed `game_insider_round` row. Host then re-clicks START on the lobby; `start_insider_round` computes the next round_number via `coalesce(max(round_number), 0) + 1` (already forward-compatible from US-044). Phase + status guards (`phase in ('reveal', 'result_failed')` AND `status = 'PLAYING'`) make the RPC a no-op in any other state — concurrent fires by 4 reveal-screen clients collapse safely (one writer wins via `where status = 'PLAYING'`). Returns `int` for "rows affected" (per US-047 pattern). This is the minimum-correct multi-round path; future Phase-6 work can replace with auto-advance to round N+1 (re-shuffle roles + new secret in the same RPC) without touching app code.
- **Reveal screen mounts and fires `advance_to_reveal` itself (per US-062):** `cast_vote` auto-flips phase voting→reveal when the final eligible voter casts (per US-048 / migration 0027) but does NOT apply scoring — `advance_to_reveal` (migration 0028/0032) is the canonical "compute the scoreboard" RPC. The `<Reveal/>` screen's mount-time effect calls `advanceToReveal(supabase, { roomId, round })` and silently catches errors (other clients may have advanced first; the SELECT FOR UPDATE in 0032 makes concurrent fires safe). The fix surfaced by US-062's e2e: without this mount-time fire, `players.total_score` is 0 even though `phase = 'reveal'`. Reusable for any phase where (a) the phase-flip is driven by a different RPC (cast_vote / mark_correct_guess / etc.) but (b) the side effects (scoring, leaderboard write, achievement unlock) belong to a separate "compute" RPC: have the destination screen own the computation fire on mount.
- **Voting screen owns the guessed→voting auto-advance via T-3.B "anyone advances" RPC (per US-061 / Phase 5b.6):** `mark_correct_guess` (migration 0025) flips phase to `'guessed'` AND pre-populates `vote_deadline = now() + 60s` + `eligible_voter_ids` snapshot in one UPDATE — so the guessed→voting transition is a phase-only flip with no extra inputs. Migration `0030_advance_to_voting.sql` exposes a thin RPC (the standard skeleton: `perform reconcile_round_phase` → membership check (`PGAME11`/`PG011`) → `update ... where phase = 'guessed'`). The `<Voting/>` screen is mounted by the room shell for BOTH `'guessed'` and `'voting'` phases; on entering `'guessed'` every client schedules a `setTimeout(GUESSED_CELEBRATION_MS = 2000ms)` that fires `advance_to_voting` — first writer wins, the WHERE-clause idempotency makes concurrent fires safe. So no host-only gating, no client elected-leader pattern, no extra realtime metadata. Reusable for any future "celebratory pause then auto-advance" phase edge: pre-populate next-phase state at the trigger RPC, then have the next screen own its own self-timed advance via the standard "anyone advances" idempotent RPC. Phase routing in `lobby.tsx` extends the existing `roundPhase ===` chain — the shell renders `<Voting initialPhase={roundPhase}/>` so the screen knows to start in celebration mode without an extra DB roundtrip.
- **D6 hidden-tally enforcement is structural, not visual (per US-061):** the spec "NO per-player vote tallies during voting" is enforced by NEVER rendering a per-player tally testid in `<Voting/>`. e2e asserts `getByTestId(/^vote-tally-/)` count is 0 — same anti-cheat bulletproofing as US-060's D2 hint asymmetry. Group progress is rendered via a single `voting-progress` testid showing `cappedCount / eligibleCount voted`. The vote count comes from a `select count from game_insider_votes` (no per-target breakdown) so even an inspect-network attempt sees only the total. The vote_count is capped at eligibleCount on the client (`Math.min`) since `cast_vote` is server-side eligibility-filtered and the displayed denominator is never inflated. Reusable for any anti-herding "private aggregate count without per-item breakdown" UI.
- **Vote selection toggle uses self-vote sentinel for de-select (per US-061):** `cast_vote` requires a non-null `voted_player_id` (the column has a FK), so a "tap-same-card-to-deselect" gesture cannot be a NULL update. Pattern: re-tapping the currently-selected card calls `cast_vote(p_voted_player_id = mePlayerId)` — i.e. the voter votes for themselves as a sentinel meaning "deselected". On the voting screen self-cards are `disabled={isMeRow}` so a player cannot SELECT themselves directly; the only path to self-vote is the toggle-deselect, and the UI re-reads it as `selectedTarget === null`. Audit trail still reflects last intent (`voted_at` bumped on conflict). On reload, `selectedTarget` is restored from the prior vote row, treating self-vote as `null`. Reusable for any "tap-toggle on a NOT NULL FK" voting/selection UI.
- **vote-target-card lives in packages/ui (per US-061):** added `<VoteTargetCard joinOrder displayName selected disabled testId onTap />` mirroring the `PlayerChip` tag-color palette. Selected state = `ring-4 ring-goal ring-offset-2 ring-offset-ink` + absolute-positioned ✓ overlay. Min-height 120px enforced via `min-h-[120px]` (asserted via `getBoundingClientRect().height >= 120` in e2e). Disabled when not eligible OR when the card represents the voter themselves. Reusable for any cross-game voting/selection UI; shape parallels existing `PlayerChip` so the lobby chip and voting card stay visually consistent.
- **Non-Master asking view shared by Insider + Common; D2 hint is structural-asymmetric (per US-060 / Phase 5b.5c):** `apps/insider/app/room/[code]/asking-other.tsx` is one component used for BOTH Insider and Common. Identical DOM EXCEPT the Insider-only D2 hint (`asking-other-insider-hint`), which only renders when `role === "insider"` (JSX-level branch — Common's tree never includes the testid at all, count=0). Hint visibility flips via `data-state="visible|hidden"` + opacity transition based on `(now - lastActivityMs)/1000 >= 30`, where `lastActivityMs = max(latest response.created_at, round.started_at)`. So the silence threshold anchors at the round start (shared by all clients), not at component-mount-time — a player who reloads gets the hint when group has been silent, not after another 30s reset. Realtime channel name `asking-other-${roomId}-${round}-${role}` is per-role-suffixed so concurrent Insider + Common subscriptions don't collide. Reusable for any role-asymmetric subtle-affordance UI: keep the asymmetric element JSX-conditional rather than CSS-hidden so the DOM contract is anti-cheat-bulletproof and test-assertable via testid count.
- **D4 anti-cheat parity assertion strips the legitimate asymmetric element (per US-060 update to US-058 spec):** when introducing a deliberate role-asymmetric DOM element (here, the Insider-only D2 hint) while preserving overall D4 visual parity, the existing parity assertion ("Insider shell text === Common shell text") doesn't get weakened — it gets refined to "DOM identical EXCEPT for the legitimate asymmetric element". Implementation pattern: `page.evaluate(sel => { const clone = document.querySelector(sel).cloneNode(true); clone.querySelectorAll('[data-testid="<asymmetric-id>"]').forEach(el => el.remove()); return clone.textContent.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim(); })` — clone, strip the asymmetric testid, compare. Reusable any time a future story introduces an intentional D4 carve-out.
- **Asking-phase role router delegates Master to its own component (per US-059 / Phase 5b.5b):** `apps/insider/app/room/[code]/asking-phase.tsx` now does a parallel `select role from game_insider_roles` AND `select started_at, time_limit_s from game_insider_round` on mount, then either: (a) renders `<AskingMaster roomId round mePlayerId secret startedAt timeLimitS />` if role==='master' AND the secret RPC succeeded, OR (b) renders the inline non-Master shell (D4-parity for Insider+Common — same DOM, US-060 will replace with full Screen 6b). Both branches keep `data-testid="asking-phase-shell"` so US-058 asymmetric-privacy assertions hold. Master also keeps `data-testid="master-asking-secret-reminder"` (US-058 invariant). The `started_at`/`time_limit_s` columns are anon-readable on `game_insider_round` per migration 0017 column-list grant — drives the client-side countdown without an extra RPC. Reusable shape for any future per-phase router that needs to fetch round metadata once at mount and dispatch to a per-role component. Realtime subscription on `game_insider_responses` lives inside `AskingMaster` (not the parent shell) so US-060's non-Master feed can own its own subscription independently. Timer "turns red <30s" is `text-error` when `remainingS < 30`; mm:ss is rendered with `tabular-nums` to prevent jitter.
- **Insider round-phase routing in the room shell (per US-058 / Phase 5b.5a):** `apps/insider/app/room/[code]/lobby.tsx` now subscribes to `game_insider_round` filtered by `room_id=eq.<id>` (added to the existing `useRoomRealtime` tables array) and tracks `roundPhase` state, plus an initial `select phase` fetch keyed on `room.status === 'PLAYING' && room.current_round` to cover (a) the gap between subscribing and the first realtime payload and (b) a player who reloads mid-round. The render branch is: `room.status === 'PLAYING'` → if `roundPhase === 'asking'` render `<AskingPhase/>`, otherwise (preparing or null) render `<RoleReveal/>`. Future per-phase screens (voting, reveal) extend by adding more `roundPhase === '<phase>'` branches in the same place. The `game_insider_round` realtime publication is column-list (migration 0017): `phase` is broadcast but `secret_value` is NOT, so an anon subscriber gets the phase flip without leaking the secret.
- **Insider room-shell phase routing lives in `lobby.tsx` (per US-055 / Phase 5b.4a):** the existing `Lobby` component (`apps/insider/app/room/[code]/lobby.tsx`) is also the per-room state machine. Once `room.status === 'PLAYING'` is observed via the rooms Realtime subscription, it delegates rendering to `<RoleReveal roomId={room.id} round={room.current_round ?? 1} mePlayerId={me.player_id} />` instead of `<LobbyView/>`. This keeps the room-level state ownership (room/players/me + realtime) in one place rather than fanning each phase screen out into its own page route. US-058 extended this with `game_insider_round` subscription + `roundPhase`-keyed branch (see top pattern). The `Lobby` filename is now slightly misleading — it's effectively the room shell — but renaming is left for a future cleanup story to keep changes scoped.
- **Insider role-reveal: read role from `game_insider_roles`, secret via RPC (per US-055 / wireframe 5a):** `apps/insider/app/room/[code]/role-reveal.tsx` does a single `select role from game_insider_roles where (room_id, round_number, player_id)` to determine which variant to render (anon SELECT is allowed per A1.C shape (1) — pattern note 29). Master and Insider then call `getMyInsiderSecret(supabase, ...)` (the SECURITY DEFINER RPC from migration 0021) to fetch `secret_value`, which is column-RLS-protected on `game_insider_round` (anon direct SELECT denied, see pattern note 30). Common players don't need a secret. The secret is rendered uppercased in CSS-and-textContent (we apply `.toUpperCase()` inline so Playwright's `toContainText` matches the visible string regardless of CSS). Reusable for any future asymmetric-secret reveal screen across games (mafia roles, hidden cards, etc.).
- **Tag-pink secret card asserted via computed bg color in e2e (per US-055):** the wireframe spec calls for the BIG NAME card on a tag-pink background (`bg-tag-pink` Tailwind utility = `var(--color-tag-pink)` = `#ec4899` = `rgb(236, 72, 153)`). The e2e asserts the rendered color via `await secretCard.evaluate((el) => getComputedStyle(el).backgroundColor)` and `expect(bg).toBe("rgb(236, 72, 153)")`. This binds the visual contract to the test rather than relying on a class-name match (which would silently pass if Tailwind dropped the class). Reusable pattern for any wireframe-locked color treatment: assert the computed RGB rather than the className. Caveat: only works for tokens defined in the app's `globals.css` `@theme inline` block; if a token is renamed/retoned, update the test.
- **Map a Playwright multi-context page → assigned random role via localStorage probe (per US-055):** when a server RPC randomly assigns roles to N players (here `start_insider_round` shuffles via `array_agg(... order by random())` per pattern note 28), the e2e doesn't know up front which page got which role. Pattern: BEFORE the RPC fires, capture each page's `localStorage.getItem('insider_player_id')` (Insider's namespace; cross-game equivalents follow the `<namespace>_player_id` convention from `packages/core/player-id.ts`). AFTER the RPC, query `game_insider_roles` via the service role to find the assigned `player_id` for the role under test, then `pages.find(p => p.playerId === assignedPlayerId)`. Drives the asserted CTA on the correct page. Reusable for any future role-asymmetric e2e (Master view US-056, Common view US-057, future hidden-info games).
- **Cross-game `PlayerChip` + `RoomCodeDisplay` in `@social-hub/ui` (per US-054 / Phase 5b.3):** the lobby player roster UI lives in `packages/ui/src/player-chip.tsx` (server-renderable `<li>` with tag-color background indexed by `joinOrder`, Stadium Energy tokens, isMe ribbon) and `packages/ui/src/room-code-display.tsx` (client component because it owns clipboard state). Both are exported from `@social-hub/ui` (no peer-dep churn — they only need React + Tailwind classes already provided by the package). New game apps can drop these in for any LOBBY-style screen instead of re-implementing chips. The `joinOrder` → tag-color mapping `((joinOrder - 1) % 8) + 1` clamps to the 8 Stadium Energy player tag colors so any roster of arbitrary size cycles through them. Headball's lobby still inlines its own chips today; future cleanup can swap it to `@social-hub/ui` `PlayerChip` for a single source of truth.
- **Insider lobby join-form gate (per US-054 / Phase 5b.3):** the Insider lobby (`apps/insider/app/room/[code]/lobby.tsx`) is one component that branches on whether the local player is in `players` for this room. If `readPlayerId()` returns null OR the matching `players.player_id` row isn't there, render `JoinView` (display-name input + JOIN button → `joinInsiderRoomAction` → cross-game `join_room` RPC). Otherwise render `LobbyView` (player chips + START GAME). This shape works because `join_room` (migration 0002) is fully cross-game — it operates on `rooms` + `players`, knows nothing about Headball-specific tables. Insider just gets a thin server-action wrapper (`apps/insider/app/actions/join-insider-room.ts`) that maps the same P0001/P0002/P0003/P0004 codes to Thai messages. Reusable for any future game's lobby — drop in the same `Lobby` component shape and just wire game-specific START to your own RPC.
- **`startInsiderRoundAction` looks up `game_insider_room_config` server-side (per US-054):** the lobby's START GAME button doesn't know the host's chosen pack/time-limit (those live on the extension table per US-053). So `startInsiderRoundAction` does a server-side `select pack_slug, time_limit_s from game_insider_room_config where room_id = ?` then passes them to the RPC. Pattern keeps the client thin (just need `roomId` + `playerId`) and avoids round-tripping the config through `useState`. Reusable for any future game where per-room settings are stored in an extension table the lobby reads at start time.
- **T-3.B "anyone can advance" in practice (per US-054 / Phase 5b.3):** the design-doc T-3.B "any connected player can advance phase actions" applies to in-round transitions (`advance_to_asking`, `cast_vote`'s auto-advance, `advance_to_reveal`, future `advance_to_next_round`). It does NOT apply to `start_insider_round` — that RPC is host-only by design (LOBBY → PLAYING is a once-per-game transition that benefits from a single coordinator). The lobby UI shows the START GAME button to ALL players (no `isHost` gate at the UI layer) per the wireframe affordance, but a non-host click surfaces PGAME12 ("เฉพาะโฮสต์เริ่มเกมได้") via `startInsiderRoundAction.mapError`. Tests have the host click since happy-path passes the host-only check. Reusable distinction: T-3.B is for *within-round* phase transitions, NOT for room-status transitions.
- **`createRoomWithRetry` is now multi-RPC (per US-053 / packages/core):** the helper in `packages/core/src/room-code.ts` accepts an optional third arg that can be either a number (legacy `maxRetries`) or a `CreateRoomWithRetryOptions` object with `{ rpcName?: string, maxRetries?: number }`. Default rpcName is `"create_room"` (Headball) so existing call sites are unchanged. Insider's `createInsiderRoom` wrapper passes `{ rpcName: "create_insider_room" }` to retry on the same 23505 unique-violation collision behavior. Reusable for any future game's room-create RPC: write a SECURITY DEFINER `create_<game>_room` returning `(code, player_id)`, then a TS wrapper calling `createRoomWithRetry(supabase, { p_*: ... }, { rpcName: "create_<game>_room" })`. Don't fork the helper per game.
- **`game_insider_room_config` extension table for Insider room settings (per US-053 / Phase 5b.2):** the host's per-room Insider settings (`pack_slug`, `time_limit_s`, `round_count`) live in a sibling `game_insider_room_config` table keyed on `room_id`, not on `rooms` itself. Reasons: (1) keeps `rooms` game-agnostic — only `game_type` distinguishes which game owns the row (per design doc D3); (2) keeps the Phase 5 changes additive vs. Headball schema (per C4 / FR-3.4); (3) future games that need different config get their own `game_<game>_room_config` table without bloating `rooms`. The extension table is `-- no-realtime` because the config row is written exactly once at room creation and never mutated; lobby/start-round screens fetch via `select` once on mount. Reusable for any future game-specific per-room settings.
- **PGAME20 = "invalid insider room args" (per US-053 / create_insider_room):** binding assignment for range/length checks on the `create_insider_room` RPC. `time_limit_s` must be 180/300/420; `round_count` must be 1..10; `display_name` must be 1..20 chars; `host_player_id` is required. Raise with `using errcode = 'PG020'` and message prefix `'PGAME20: '`. App-side handler matches on `error.code === 'PG020'`. The form constrains these client-side, but the server-side gate protects against direct-RPC abuse and surfaces clean typed errors. Reusable shape for any future "validate input args at the start of an RPC" check: errcode for invalid args, FK + check-constraint catches if the value passes the gate but conflicts with the schema.
- **apps/insider Tailwind v4 + Stadium Energy bootstrap (per US-053 / Phase 5b.2):** when an app first ships UI (was placeholder before), it needs the full Tailwind v4 + tokens stack mirroring Hub: (a) devDependencies: `@tailwindcss/postcss`, `tailwindcss`, `@playwright/test`, `eslint`, `eslint-config-next`; (b) dependencies: `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `tw-animate-css`, `shadcn`, `zod`, `@supabase/ssr`, `@social-hub/content`, `@social-hub/types`; (c) `app/globals.css` with `@import "tailwindcss"` + `@import "tw-animate-css"` + `@import "shadcn/tailwind.css"` + `@source "../../../packages/ui/src"` + the full `@theme inline { ... }` Stadium Energy token block (copy from `apps/hub/app/globals.css`, NOT `apps/headball/app/globals.css` which has a heavier shadcn token set the bare app doesn't need); (d) `app/layout.tsx` loads Anton + Bebas_Neue + IBM_Plex_Sans_Thai_Looped via `next/font/google`, sets the html className to `${anton.variable} ${bebas.variable} ${plexThai.variable}`, body className to `bg-ink text-on-dark font-body`; (e) `apps/insider/.env.local` is required for `next dev` to bind Supabase (Next loads `.env.local` from the project dir, NOT workspace root) — copy from `apps/headball/.env.local`; (f) `tsconfig.json` adds `paths: { "@/*": ["./*"], "@social-hub/*": ["../../packages/*/src/index.ts"] }`. The postcss config at workspace root is auto-discovered by `postcss-load-config` walking up the dir tree; no per-app postcss.config.mjs needed.
- **Stale next-server processes cause Playwright `webServer.reuseExistingServer:true` to silently use the wrong app (per US-053):** when previous test runs leave next-server processes bound to ports 3000/3001/3002 from before a code change, Playwright's `reuseExistingServer:true` will skip its spawn step and use those stale servers — which serve the OLD app code. Symptom: e2e tests time out at navigation or hit unexpected 404s. Fix: `pkill -f "next-server" && pkill -f "next dev" && pkill -f "turbo run dev" && pkill -f "bun run dev"` before re-running the suite. The cold-compile + stale-server combo is the most likely cause of "passed alone, fails in suite" Playwright reports for this monorepo. Reusable check: `lsof -i :3000 -i :3001 -i :3002` should show ZERO listeners before invoking `bunx playwright test`.
- **`scored_at` idempotency stamp for Insider scoring (per US-049 / advance_to_reveal):** when a transition into a terminal state needs side effects (here: bumping `players.total_score`) but other RPCs (cast_vote/US-048) might already have advanced the *phase* via their own auto-advance, the phase column is NOT a sufficient idempotency guard. Pattern: add a separate timestamp column (`game_insider_round.scored_at`) that is NULL until the side-effecting work runs, and stamped when it does. The function early-returns `if v_scored_at is not null` before touching anything else, then performs the work, then stamps `scored_at = now()` last (with a `where … and scored_at is null` guard so concurrent calls also can't double-apply). Reusable for any future "transition + apply side effects exactly once" pattern (e.g., cross-round scoring rollup, end-of-game leaderboard write, achievement unlocks).
- **Three-branch outcome RPC for terminal phase (per US-049 / advance_to_reveal):** when a "compute the result" RPC must converge on `phase=reveal` (or its equivalent terminal state) from multiple upstream phases, structure the body as: (1) reconcile_round_phase chain → self-heal expired deadlines; (2) idempotency early-return on the stamped timestamp; (3) handle the *failure-mode* branch first (e.g., result_failed → no scoring, just stamp and return); (4) handle the *transition* branch (voting → reveal) with the called-too-early no-op check (vote count < eligible count AND deadline future); (5) compute the *success* outcome (scoring) with the side-effect timestamp written last. The order matters: failure branch first means we don't try to score a round where there were no votes; transition branch has a "called early" no-op so client UIs can fire the RPC freely on a timer without triggering bad behavior.
- **Optional realtime publication for derived columns (per US-049 / scored_at):** when adding a column to a table that already has a column-list publication (game_insider_round per migration 0017), you can opt OUT of broadcasting the new column. The CHANGE: `alter table … add column scored_at timestamptz` succeeds without touching the publication; the column simply isn't in the broadcast. Clients still see other column changes (notably `phase`) which serve as their cue to refetch. This avoids the churn of `alter publication … drop table; alter publication … add table (…)` (PG cannot edit a column-list publication entry in place — only swap the whole entry). Reusable for any future column whose value is derived state clients can re-query for, rather than something they need pushed.
- **Tally-with-tie SQL pattern (per US-049 / advance_to_reveal):** to find "all tied at the maximum vote count" in plpgsql, use a two-step CTE-with-aggregate pattern: (1) compute the tally via a CTE grouped by `voted_player_id`; (2) `select max(c) into v_top_count from tally`; (3) re-issue the same CTE and `select array_agg(voted_player_id) into v_top_voted from tally where c = v_top_count`. This is necessary because plpgsql CTEs don't survive across statements — each `select` is its own statement and re-runs the CTE definition. Once you have `v_top_voted uuid[]`, downstream logic like "is the insider in the tied set?" reduces to `v_insider_id = any(v_top_voted)`. Cleaner than DENSE_RANK or window functions for this size of data and this clarity of intent.
- **Auto-advance phase from inside a mutation RPC (per US-048 / cast_vote):** when an RPC's mutation is the trigger for a phase transition (cast_vote: last vote → voting→reveal; future analogues: last response, last action of any phase), the phase flip happens INSIDE the same RPC call as the mutation. Pattern: (a) UPSERT the row first, (b) re-`select count(*)` from the just-mutated table joined against the eligibility array (`where voter_player_id = any(v_eligible)`), (c) `update game_insider_round set phase = '<next>' where … and phase = '<current>'` — the WHERE-clause phase guard makes it idempotent if multiple final-mutation calls race. The downstream "advance" RPC (advance_to_reveal) becomes a no-op for transition but still computes scores idempotently. This avoids a separate poll-then-advance RTT on the happy path while keeping the explicit advance path available for timeout. Reusable for any future phase-transition driven by the count of mutation rows reaching the eligibility-array length.
- **Three-tier phase guard with post-reconcile expiry distinction (per US-048 / cast_vote, US-046 / mark_correct_guess, US-045 / master_respond):** voting/asking-phase RPCs use the same three-tier guard order after `perform reconcile_round_phase`: (1) post-reconcile expiry path — if the phase column was just flipped by reconcile (e.g., voting→reveal because vote_deadline passed, or asking→result_failed because asking deadline passed), surface a deadline-specific code (PGAME19 for vote, PGAME02 for asking) so the UI can render a timeout state distinct from a generic phase mismatch. The discriminator is the post-reconcile phase column being the next-phase-name AND the relevant deadline column being in the past. (2) Authorization (master-only check or eligibility check). (3) Generic phase guard — any other non-target phase rejects with the role-specific code (PGAME16 for asking-only, PGAME18 for voting-only). Reusable for any future timer-bounded phase RPC.
- **PG transaction rollback wipes reconcile when RPC raises (per US-048 / cast_vote, US-046 / mark_correct_guess):** `perform reconcile_round_phase(...)` in the first line of an RPC commits its UPDATE only if the RPC returns successfully. If the RPC raises (post-reconcile expiry path: PGAME02 / PGAME19), the entire function-call transaction rolls back, INCLUDING the reconcile UPDATE. The phase column thus stays at its pre-reconcile value (e.g., 'asking' or 'voting') even though the RPC's error message says the deadline passed. Tests must call `reconcile_round_phase` explicitly as a SECOND RPC call to actually commit the phase advance and verify it. This is fine in production because the next time ANY RPC runs (or the client explicitly calls reconcile / expire_round), the phase will advance idempotently. Don't try to "fix" this with savepoints — PL/pgSQL exception blocks act as savepoints but `raise` rolls through them too.
- **Public-facing reconcile slice + helper coexistence (per US-047 / A3.B):** `expire_round(p_room_id, p_round)` is the single-purpose, public-facing RPC clients fire the moment their local asking-phase timer hits zero. It does ONE transition: `asking → result_failed` when the deadline has passed. It deliberately does NOT subsume `reconcile_round_phase` (migration 0020) — the helper still runs as the first line of every Insider mutation RPC and additionally handles `voting → reveal`. So the pattern is: clients call `expire_round` directly when their timer expires (broad fan-in, idempotent via WHERE-clause phase guard); inside any other RPC, the implicit `perform reconcile_round_phase(...)` chain absorbs the same transition AND the voting one. Don't merge the two — keep `expire_round` minimal and focused on what clients need to fire on a timer event. Reusable for any future "timer-driven public RPC" alongside an internal reconciler (e.g., a hypothetical `expire_voting` if voting eventually needs a public-facing form too — though today voting timeout is handled inside `advance_to_reveal`).
- **`returns int` for "rows affected" idempotency contracts (per US-047):** when an RPC's WHERE-clause guard is the idempotency mechanism (so two concurrent calls produce one winner + one no-op), declare it `returns int` and `get diagnostics v_count = row_count; return v_count;` after the UPDATE. Tests can then assert `a + b === 1` for two concurrent calls — strong evidence that exactly one mutation occurred. Cheaper and more truthful than re-reading the row to infer the count. Reusable for any future "first-fires-wins" RPC where the SQL UPDATE is the natural single source of truth (e.g., race-on-vote scenarios that don't need PK uniqueness violations).
- **Insider round bootstrap pattern (per US-044 / Phase 5a.7):** the canonical "host-starts-game" RPC for any new game (Insider's `start_insider_round`, future `start_<game>_round` analogues) follows a five-step shape: (1) `select … into v_host, v_status from rooms where id = p_room_id for update` — single locked read for both authorization and status gate; (2) authorization gate `if v_host <> p_player_id then raise PGAMExx using errcode = 'PGxxx'`; (3) `if v_status <> 'LOBBY' then raise …` — LOBBY-only entry; (4) `select count(*), array_agg(player_id order by random()) into v_count, v_player_ids from players where room_id = p_room_id` shuffles the roster in a single pass — slots [1..k] become the privileged roles, [k+1..N] become commons; (5) insert per-round state + per-player role rows via `generate_subscripts(v_player_ids, 1)`, then `update rooms set status = 'PLAYING', current_round = v_round_number`. The `for update` lock + status-flip combine to make the RPC effectively single-shot per game (a concurrent second call sees PLAYING and raises). Reusable for any future game that has a "host clicks Start" entry point.
- **Random shuffle in plpgsql via `array_agg(... order by random())`:** for assigning N players to ordered roles (master, insider, commons), do NOT loop with `random()`-based PL/pgSQL: a single `select array_agg(player_id order by random()) into v_player_ids from players where …` shuffles the entire roster in one pass. Then map roles by array index: `v_player_ids[1] = master`, `v_player_ids[2] = insider`, etc. Iterate the array via `generate_subscripts(arr, 1) as g(i)` in an INSERT...SELECT for the role rows. Idiomatic, deterministic per call (no read-then-update races), and works for any role-assignment shape. Reusable for any future "shuffle and assign" Insider/multi-role game RPC.
- **Public Insider sub-tables vs. secret-bearing tables (per US-040 / A1.C):** Insider per-round data splits cleanly into two RLS shapes. (1) Tables that carry NO asymmetric secret (`game_insider_roles`, `game_insider_responses`, `game_insider_votes`) use the simple shape: `enable row level security` + `for select to anon using (true)` + `alter publication supabase_realtime add table <t>` (no column list, no REVOKE/GRANT dance). Knowing roles/responses/votes does NOT leak the secret — the secret stays on `game_insider_round.secret_value` only. (2) Tables that DO carry an asymmetric secret (only `game_insider_round` so far) use the full column-level pattern from migration 0017. When adding a new Insider per-round table: default to shape (1) unless the table holds something only Master/Insider should ever read.
- **Column-level secret protection (per US-038 / A1.C):** for asymmetric-secret game tables (Insider's `secret_value`, future Master/Spy reveals), the pattern is: (a) create the table with all columns inline (NO split table), (b) `enable row level security` + permissive `for select to anon using (true)` policy, (c) `revoke select on <table> from anon` to strip Supabase's default broad grant, (d) `grant select (col1, col2, ...) on <table> to anon` enumerating ONLY non-secret columns, (e) Realtime publication MUST use explicit column list `alter publication supabase_realtime add table <t> (col1, col2, ...)` because column-level GRANTs do NOT filter Realtime payloads — without the column list, anon subscribers receive `secret_value` over the WebSocket. Master/Insider fetch the secret via SECURITY DEFINER RPC (e.g. `get_my_insider_secret`). Test signature: anon `select('*')` returns `error.code === '42501'` (insufficient_privilege), anon `select('secret_value')` also returns 42501, anon `select('non_secret_col1, non_secret_col2, ...')` returns the row WITHOUT secret_value. Apps's lint gate (`scripts/check-realtime-publication.sh`) tolerates the column-list form because the regex captures the table name and ignores the trailing `(...)`.
- **apps/insider vitest scaffolding (per US-038):** apps/insider gets the same minimal vitest setup as apps/headball: a `vitest.config.ts` at app root with `environment: "node"` (no jsdom — these are Postgres integration tests, not React render tests), `include: ["lib/**/*.test.ts"]`, `exclude: ["e2e/**", "node_modules/**", ".next/**"]`, single `@` alias resolving to the app root. Add `vitest` (devDependency) + `@supabase/supabase-js` (dependency) to `apps/insider/package.json`. The workspace-root `vitest.config.ts` already lists `apps/*` so the new config is auto-discovered. Reuse the local-Supabase fallback URL/anon-key/service-role pattern from `apps/headball/lib/__tests__/word-packs.test.ts` (stable demo JWTs across every developer machine).
- **content_packs slug for Insider integration tests (per US-038):** when an Insider integration test needs a valid `pack_slug` to satisfy the FK on `game_insider_round.pack_slug → content_packs.slug`, use `football-premier-league` — it's seeded by migration 0013 and exists in every `bunx supabase db reset` run. Don't seed a test-only content_pack; the FK satisfaction is incidental to the assertion. The `insider-thai-food` slug only exists after `bun run scripts/seed-insider-packs.ts` runs (NOT part of `db reset`), so it's unreliable for migration tests.
- **Cross-subdomain redirect e2e pattern (per US-034):** when a hub-side flow redirects to a per-game subdomain (`http://headball.localhost:<port>/room/<code>`), Chromium resolves `*.localhost` to `127.0.0.1`, so the navigation lands back on the same dev server. The Playwright spec uses `context.route(/headball\..*\/room\/[A-Z0-9]{6}$/, route.fulfill(200))` to stub the cross-origin response, then `page.waitForURL(/headball\..*\/room\/<code>/)` and `expect(page.url()).toContain("headball.")`. The `NEXT_PUBLIC_HEADBALL_URL` (and `_INSIDER_URL`) env vars are set in `apps/hub/playwright.config.ts` `webServer.env` so the spawned dev server bundles the `headball.localhost:<port>` form. Reusable for US-035 (lookup-room) and any future hub-side cross-subdomain redirect tests.
- Root `package.json` uses Bun workspaces (`apps/*`, `packages/*`). `apps/` and `packages/` dirs do not need to exist for `bun install` to succeed — empty globs are tolerated.
- Existing Headball app code lives at repo root through Phase 0; Phase 1 relocates it to `apps/headball/`.
- Turborepo v2 requires `packageManager` field in root `package.json` to resolve workspaces (currently `bun@1.1.29`).
- Turbo v2 schema uses `tasks` (not `pipeline`) — see `turbo.json`. Build outputs include `.next/**` (excluding cache) and `dist/**` for libraries.
- Bun 1.1.29 generates `bun.lockb` (binary), not `bun.lock` — turbo emits a harmless WARNING about parsing the lockfile but still functions; upgrading to Bun ≥1.1.42 (text lockfile) would silence it.
- Pre-existing tech debt: root `bun run lint` reports thousands of errors (eslint scope issue / type-aware rules in deps) — predates Phase 0 work, not a regression. Phase 1 relocation will likely scope eslint to `apps/headball/` and reduce the surface.
- Workspace packages under `packages/*` declare `"main": "./src/index.ts"`, `"types": "./src/index.ts"`, and `"exports": { ".": "./src/index.ts" }` so consumers import TS source directly (no build step) — matches D3 in the design doc.
- `next lint` is removed in Next 16 — do NOT add `"lint": "next lint"` to per-app `package.json`. Use `eslint` directly when wiring per-app lint (Phase 0.4/0.7).
- `.turbo/` (per-package turbo cache + logs) is gitignored at the repo root — covers every workspace package without repeating in each.
- Shared TS config lives at `tsconfig.base.json` (root). Apps/packages extend via `"extends": "../../tsconfig.base.json"`. Base owns `target/module/strict/jsx/baseUrl/paths`; consumers add only Next plugin + `include`. Don't redeclare `paths` per-package.
- `@social-hub/*` path alias resolves to `packages/*/src/index.ts` (set in base). Consume with `import { X } from "@social-hub/core"` — no deep imports (matches D3).
- Root `tsconfig.json` (existing Headball app) does NOT extend base by design — it stays Next-13-style for the legacy root app until Phase 1 relocates Headball into `apps/headball/` and that app gets its own base-extending tsconfig.
- `bunx tsc --noEmit` from a sub-dir (e.g. `cd apps/hub && bunx tsc --noEmit`) picks up that workspace's tsconfig — useful for verifying per-package extends work.
- `data/` directory stays at workspace root through Phase 1 (root `scripts/build-seed.ts` resolves it via `path.join(ROOT, "data")` where ROOT = `..` from scripts/). `apps/headball/tsconfig.json` adds `@/data/*` → `../../data/*` so headball code can keep using `import { ... } from "@/data/premier-league"` after the relocation without changing imports.
- `vitest.setup.ts` was moved into `apps/headball/` during US-008 (not just US-009 territory). Reason: `bunx tsc --noEmit` from `apps/headball/` needs to compile a file that imports `@testing-library/jest-dom/vitest` so Vitest's `Assertion` interface is augmented with `toBeInTheDocument`/`toHaveTextContent`. Without that file inside the project's tsconfig include, every test file using those matchers fails typecheck.
- After Phase 1.1 (US-008), root `package.json` keeps only workspace tooling: `turbo`, `typescript`, `supabase` (CLI used at workspace root), `@types/node` (for scripts/*.ts). Every Next/React/test library lives in `apps/headball/package.json`.
- Root scripts (`dev`, `build`, `lint`, `test`) are now Turbo proxies (`turbo run <task> --filter=@social-hub/headball` for the single-app tasks). Workspace-root `next dev` won't work after Phase 1.1 because `next.config.ts` / `tsconfig.json` still live at root through US-009 — by design.
- `apps/headball/tsconfig.json` paths order: list more-specific patterns first (`@/data/*` before `@/*`). TS resolves longest-prefix-match regardless of order, but listing in specificity order reads cleaner and avoids confusion.
- After Phase 1.2 (US-009), workspace root has NO `tsconfig.json`. `bunx tsc --noEmit` from workspace root prints help text (no project found) — that's expected. Always run typecheck per-package: `cd apps/headball && bunx tsc --noEmit`. CI/turbo will eventually wire this via per-app `typecheck` scripts.
- After Phase 1.2, headball seed scripts at `apps/headball/scripts/*.ts` resolve workspace root via `join(HERE, "..", "..", "..")` (was `..`). Workspace `data/` and `supabase/` directories stay at workspace root for ALL games to share — see `data/seed/categories.json` and `supabase/migrations/`. Root `package.json` `seed:*` scripts now point to `apps/headball/scripts/*.ts`.
- `apps/headball/vitest.config.ts` declares aliases as an ARRAY (not object) so a regex matcher (`@/data/*` → workspace root `data/`) can come before the catch-all `@` → `apps/headball/`. Vitest object-form aliases don't support regex; array form does. Keep order: most-specific first.
- After Phase 1.2, `apps/headball/lib/__tests__/categories.test.ts` resolves the categories.json fixture via `join(__dirname, "..", "..", "..", "..", "data", "seed", ...)` — four `..`s because `__tests__` is four levels deep under workspace root.
- `apps/headball/.env.local` is required for `bunx playwright test` from `apps/headball/`. Next.js loads `.env.local` from its project directory (where `next.config.ts` lives), so it's NOT inherited from workspace root. The maintainer copies (or symlinks) workspace-root `.env.local` to `apps/headball/.env.local` after first checkout. Both are gitignored.
- `postcss.config.mjs` stays at workspace root through Phase 1; PostCSS uses `postcss-load-config` which searches up the dir tree, so Tailwind v4 still works for `apps/headball/` even though the postcss config is one level above. Move it into `apps/headball/` only if a future per-app PostCSS config is needed.
- `.gitignore` patterns are NOT recursive by default — `/node_modules` only ignores root `node_modules/`. Phase 1.2 added `**/node_modules`, `**/test-results/`, `**/playwright-report/`, and `apps/*/scripts/ralph/.last-branch` so per-app scratch dirs stay untracked across the monorepo. Update this list whenever a new app is added.
- Workspace-root `vitest.config.ts` uses Vitest 4's `test.projects: ["apps/*"]` pointer so `bunx vitest run` from repo root discovers each app's own `vitest.config.ts` (currently just `apps/headball/`). Without this, naked `bunx vitest run` at root tries to globally collect all `*.test.ts` files including `.worktrees/**` and fails — the `projects` pointer constrains discovery to workspace apps. Each app still owns its plugins/aliases/setup.
- `bun run test` (workspace-root, proxies to `turbo run test`) and `bunx vitest run` (workspace-root, via `projects: ["apps/*"]`) are both valid ways to run the workspace test suite. Turbo gives caching + parallelism across apps; raw vitest gives faster startup and `--watch`. Keep both working.
- **Vercel-deferral cascade (per US-005/US-012):** until US-005 is unblocked, every story whose acceptance criteria reference a Vercel preview URL (US-012, /qa GATEs US-013/US-022/US-030, and Phase 0–3 deploy checks) must use a local equivalent: push to origin, run `bun run build` at workspace root, run `/qa standard` against `localhost:3000` (Headball), `:3001` (Hub), `:3002` (Insider). Mark the story `passes:true` with a DEFERRED note in prd.json mirroring US-005's pattern, listing exactly which Vercel-only criteria were swapped. The eventual US-005 redo sweep greps for "DEFERRED Vercel verification" in prd.json `notes` to find what to re-verify before US-031 (cross-subdomain redirects) lands.
- **Playwright cold-start flake (Next 16 Turbopack):** the first e2e spec (alphabetically: `chip-suggestions.spec.ts`) can fail at `startGameAsHost` with "Start Game" button still `disabled` when the Next dev server was just spawned by Playwright's `webServer` (cold compile). The 15s `toBeEnabled` timeout in `_helpers/flow.ts` isn't long enough to cover Turbopack's first-page compile + Realtime channel warmup. Mitigations: (a) keep `reuseExistingServer:true` and run `bun run dev` once before the suite; (b) re-run the suite a second time — second run is 14/14; (c) if it ever needs a code fix, bump `toBeEnabled` timeout in `startGameAsHost` to 30_000. NOT a code regression — production behavior unchanged. Acceptance criterion "Zero regressions vs baseline" is satisfied because the flake is environmental, not behavioral.
- **Outdated PRD test counts:** Phase 1 GATE acceptance criteria (e.g. US-013) cite specific test counts ("21/21 vitest", "5/5 playwright") frozen at PRD-write time. Phase 1.x stories add new unit + e2e tests. When the actual count is HIGHER than the criterion AND all tests pass, that satisfies the gate (strictly more coverage, zero regressions). Note the discrepancy in the prd.json `notes` for the gate so future Ralph iterations see why we considered it green.
- **packages/core game-agnostic API shape (FR-2.1):** when extracting a primitive that takes a typed Supabase Database in headball, do NOT propagate the `Database` generic into packages/core. Instead: (a) accept `SupabaseClient` (default-typed, Database=any), (b) make the RPC's args/result types call-site generics with `unknown` defaults (`<TArgs = unknown, TResult = unknown>`), (c) cast inside core (`args as Record<string, unknown>`) to satisfy supabase-js v2 rpc typing. Consumers like headball pass their own `CreateRoomArgs`/`CreateRoomResult` from `@/lib/types` at the call site. This keeps packages/core a pure cross-game primitive while preserving end-to-end type safety in apps. Pattern established by US-014 (room-code); expected to repeat for US-015 (supabase clients, accept `<TDB>` generic), US-016 (dispatch wrapper), US-018 (player-id helper).
- **packages/core test discovery:** workspace-root `vitest.config.ts` `projects` array must list `packages/core` explicitly (not the glob `packages/*`) because packages/{ui,types,content} don't yet have `vitest.config.ts`. As future packages add their own configs, append them to the projects array. Each package owns its own vitest plugins/aliases/setup; use `environment: "node"` for pure-logic packages (no jsdom).
- **Vitest project labels:** when running `bunx vitest run --reporter=verbose`, tests from workspace packages are prefixed with `|@social-hub/<name>| src/path/to/test.ts` instead of just the path. Useful sanity check that a test moved to a package is actually being collected from that package, not double-collected from the old location.
- **packages/core dispatch wrapper (per US-016 / C1.B):** every cross-game RPC call in apps goes through `dispatch<TArgs, TResult>(supabase, rpcName, args)` from `@social-hub/core`. It unwraps array results to row[0], throws `GameRpcError` with `{ code, message, context: { rpc, args } }` on failure, and parses Postgres errcode via `parsePgErrCode` (returns `"UNKNOWN"` if absent). Postgres functions raise with `using errcode = 'PGAMExx'` per ranges in `packages/core/error-codes.md`: PGAME01–09 cross-game, PGAME10–49 Insider, PGAME50–89 future, PGAME90–99 platform.
- **packages/core typing convention (FR-2.1, reaffirmed by US-014/015/016):** primitives that wrap Supabase calls (`createRoomWithRetry`, `dispatch`) accept default-typed `SupabaseClient` (Database=any) and use call-site generics for args/result with `unknown` defaults. Cast `args as Record<string, unknown>` inside core to satisfy supabase-js v2 rpc typing. Apps preserve end-to-end type safety by passing their own arg/result types at the call site. Don't propagate `Database` into core. Singleton browser clients (which need a Database type) stay app-local.
- **`"use client"` required on every React hook in packages/* (per US-017):** any file under `packages/*/src/` that imports from `"react"` (`useState`, `useEffect`, `useRef`, etc.) MUST begin with `"use client"`. Reason: `packages/core/src/index.ts` is a barrel re-export consumed by both Next server actions (`apps/*/app/actions/*.ts`) and client components. Without the directive, Next 16 RSC errors at build time: "importing a module that depends on `useRef` into a React Server Component module". The directive marks the file as a client boundary so server consumers compile through it cleanly. Add `react` to BOTH `peerDependencies` AND `devDependencies` of the package (mirror the `@supabase/ssr` pattern). Established by `useRoomRealtime`; will repeat for any future hook in core/ui.
- **Realtime/effect hook callback-ref pattern (per US-017):** when a custom hook in packages/core takes consumer callbacks (`onChange`, `onReconnect`, `onStatusChange`) and the consumer naturally passes inline `useCallback` closures, store each callback in a `useRef` and update the ref via a tiny separate `useEffect`. The main effect (channel subscribe/unsubscribe) then depends only on stable structural inputs (e.g., `[supabase, roomId, tablesKey]`), NOT the callback identities. Otherwise every render that produces a new callback identity would tear down and resubscribe the Realtime channel. For array inputs that can't be stable (e.g., `tables: {table, filter}[]`), derive a `tablesKey = tables.map(...).join("|")` string and use the string in deps. Consumer should `useMemo` the array on the relevant roomId so the strings themselves don't churn either. Reusable for future Insider round-phase subscription (T-2.A).
- **Tailwind v4 + workspace UI package source discovery (per US-019):** Tailwind v4 (`@tailwindcss/postcss`) auto-discovers utility usage from the consuming app's tree but does NOT scan workspace symlinks under `node_modules/@social-hub/*`. To make `<Button />` from `@social-hub/ui` emit its classes, every consuming app's globals.css must include an explicit `@source "../../../packages/ui/src";` directive (path relative to the CSS file). Without it, classes used only inside packages/ui are silently dropped from the bundle. There is no JS `tailwind.config.ts` to register paths in v4 — it's CSS-first. Reusable for any future workspace UI consumer (apps/hub, apps/insider).
- **Stadium Energy tokens canonical source (per US-019):** `packages/ui/src/tokens.css` is the documentation reference; the actual functional copy lives inlined in each app's `app/globals.css` inside a Tailwind v4 `@theme inline { ... }` block (Tailwind v4 doesn't yet support importing `@theme` blocks across packages). When a token changes, update BOTH places. The duplication is intentional until Tailwind ships cross-package `@theme` import support. Stadium Energy tokens defined: surfaces (ink, surface, surface-elevated, hairline*, canvas, surface-soft, surface-card), text (on-dark*, on-light*), primary action (goal, goal-active, goal-disabled), player tags (red/blue/yellow/green/purple/orange/pink/cyan), semantic (success/warning/error/info), fonts (hero=Bebas, display=Anton, body=PlexThai), animations (hb-shake, hb-flash, hb-pop).
- **packages/ui peer-dep convention (per US-019):** UI primitives package declares React, react-dom, @base-ui/react, lucide-react as `peerDependencies` (single React tree across the workspace) and bundles class-variance-authority, clsx, tailwind-merge as direct `dependencies` (utility libs, no singleton concerns). Mirror this for any future shared UI package.
- **packages/ui vitest config quirk (per US-019):** packages/ui's `vitest.config.ts` should NOT load tsx files in tests unless `@vitejs/plugin-react` is wired. Default rolldown ssrTransform fails to parse JSX. Keep package-level smoke tests on pure-TS exports (e.g., `cn` from `./utils`) — render tests belong in apps where the React plugin is already configured.
- **Realtime publication gate (per US-021 / A4):** every `create table <name>` in `supabase/migrations/*.sql` must either (a) have a matching `alter publication supabase_realtime add table <name>;` in some migration, or (b) be marked with a trailing `-- no-realtime` comment on the same line as the `create table` keyword. `scripts/check-realtime-publication.sh` enforces this and runs as the first step of `bun run lint`. When adding a new table to a migration, decide upfront: do clients need to subscribe to row events? If yes → add the publication line. If no → add `-- no-realtime`. Don't leave it ambiguous; the gate will fail.
- **content_packs slug convention (per US-023 / A2.B):** registry slugs follow `<handler-prefix>-<source-ref>`, e.g. `football-premier-league` (handler `football_category`, source_ref `premier-league`) and `insider-thai-food` (handler `word_list`, source_ref pointing at a `word_packs.slug`). Source_ref is the natural key into the underlying lookup; the prefix names the handler family. Apps NEVER query underlying tables directly — go through `get_random_pack_item(slug)` (added in US-025 / migration 0015).
- **Phase 3 additive-only rule (per US-023 / C4 / FR-3.4):** every Phase 3 migration (0013/0014/0015) is purely additive — new tables + new RPCs + new policies. NO `alter table` of existing Headball schema (`rooms`, `players`, `categories`, `category_players`, `football_players`, `round_state`, etc.). Headball runtime path is byte-for-byte identical through Phase 3. If a future story needs to modify Headball schema, that's a Phase 6+ decision, not Phase 3.
- **Integration tests against running local Supabase (per US-023 / T-2.B):** `apps/headball/lib/__tests__/*.test.ts` may hit the local Supabase API directly via `createClient(NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY)`. Defaults to `http://127.0.0.1:54321` + the dev anon key (matching `apps/headball/.env.local`). Anon-readable tables (with RLS + anon SELECT policy) make this trivial for assertions on seeded data — no test rig, no service-role key. Requires `bunx supabase start` to be running. Phase 3 stories US-024 (word_packs) and US-025 (RPC) will follow the same pattern.
- **PGAMExx → 5-char SQLSTATE mapping (per US-025 / error-codes.md):** PostgreSQL rejects 7-char errcodes (`'PGAME01'`) as unrecognized exception conditions. Established mapping: `PGAMExx` → SQLSTATE `'PGxxx'` (class `PG` unreserved by PG; subclass = symbolic suffix zero-padded to 3 digits). E.g. `PGAME01 → PG001`, `PGAME10 → PG010`. Always embed the symbolic name at the start of the message (`'PGAME01: pack not found: %'`) so both `error.code` (SQLSTATE) and `error.message` (symbolic) carry the identifier. App-side error handling matches on `error.code === 'PG001'`. Headball-legacy `P0xxx` codes stay untouched (C4); only NEW cross-game / Insider RPCs use the new mapping.
- **`returns table(...)` plpgsql + qualified column refs (per US-025):** when a function declares `returns table(item_id text, display_value text, metadata jsonb)` and the body uses `return query select … wpi.metadata`, fully-qualify EVERY column in the select list. Bare unqualified column names that match an OUT parameter name (e.g., bare `metadata`) introduce ambiguity that PL/pgSQL resolves inconsistently across versions. Always: `wpi.metadata`, `fp.id`, etc.
- **Workspace-root scripts/ TS toolchain (per US-026):** TS scripts that operate on cross-game infrastructure (content seeding, ops tooling) live at workspace-root `scripts/` (alongside `check-realtime-publication.sh`), NOT under `apps/headball/scripts/`. Add a `scripts/tsconfig.json` extending `../tsconfig.base.json` with `"types": ["node"]` so `cd scripts && bunx tsc --noEmit` typechecks them without polluting any app's tsconfig. Per-app seed scripts (Headball Wikidata pipeline) stay under `apps/<app>/scripts/`. Root `package.json` `seed:*` scripts spawn either via `bun run scripts/<name>.ts` (workspace-root) or `bun run apps/<app>/scripts/<name>.ts` (app-local).
- **Idempotent Supabase seed via supabase-js (per US-026):** for cross-table seed scripts, use `.upsert(rows, { onConflict: "<pk>", ignoreDuplicates: true })` rather than raw SQL. Supabase-js translates this to `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Composite PKs (e.g. `word_pack_items (pack_slug, value)`) take a comma-joined `onConflict` string. RLS-protected tables require the service-role key; reuse the local fallback JWT pattern from `apps/headball/e2e/_helpers/admin.ts` so the script runs out of the box against `bunx supabase start` with no env setup. Staging-target runs require `SUPABASE_URL_STAGING` + `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY_STAGING` env vars; defer until US-006 wires them.
- **Phase 4 rooms.game_type column (per US-031 / Phase 4 PRD):** `rooms` carries `game_type text not null default 'headball'` with `check (game_type in ('headball','insider'))` and a `rooms_game_type_idx` btree (cheap; hub will filter by `code` first but a small index helps future analytics). Existing Headball rows are unaffected (the default applies during the alter, so backfill is automatic). NO publication change needed — `rooms` is already in `supabase_realtime` and `alter table … add column` propagates to existing publications. The realtime-publication script only flags `create table`, so adding a column to an already-published table is invisible to it (correct behavior). When Insider lands (Phase 5+), its `create_room`-equivalent RPC must set `game_type := 'insider'`; the Headball `create_room` RPC keeps the default and needs no change (preserves C4 additive-only).
- **Database type regen — silence stderr (per US-028 / T-6 / FR-3.6):** the canonical Supabase types live at `packages/types/src/database.types.ts` and are re-exported from `@social-hub/types` (alongside `Json`). Apps import via `import type { Database } from "@social-hub/types"`. Regenerate after any migration that adds/alters a `public.*` table or function: `bunx supabase gen types typescript --local 2>/dev/null > packages/types/src/database.types.ts`. The `2>/dev/null` is REQUIRED — Supabase CLI writes startup chatter ("Saved lockfile", "Connecting to db 5432") to stderr, and Bun's redirect can interleave it into the output file, breaking the entire workspace typecheck with cryptic syntax errors at line 1. Don't import `Database` directly from a sibling app's lib dir; it's package-only now.
- **Insider RPC standard skeleton (per US-043 / T-3.B):** every Insider mutation RPC follows the same three-step skeleton: (1) `perform reconcile_round_phase(p_room_id, p_round)` — self-heal expired deadlines BEFORE inspecting phase; (2) authorization check — most commonly `if not exists (select 1 from players where room_id = p_room_id and player_id = p_player_id) then raise … using errcode = '<code>'` (PGAME11 = "not in room", SQLSTATE PG011); host-only RPCs additionally check `rooms.host_player_id = p_player_id`; (3) phase-guarded `update game_insider_round set phase = '<next>', … where … and phase = '<current>'` — the WHERE-clause phase filter is the idempotency mechanism (second call or wrong-phase call → zero rows updated → silent no-op, no error). Don't add IF/THEN branching for "phase already advanced" — let the WHERE clause absorb it. Pattern: `advance_to_asking` (preparing→asking) is the simplest instance; reused for `mark_correct_guess` (asking→guessed), `advance_to_reveal` (voting→reveal), etc.
- **PGAME11 = "player not in room" (per US-043):** binding assignment, supersedes the "already voted" example in `packages/core/error-codes.md`. Used when a SECURITY DEFINER Insider RPC needs to verify the caller is a member of `players` for the room. Membership check shape: `if not exists (select 1 from players where room_id = p_room_id and player_id = p_player_id)`. Raise with `using errcode = 'PG011'` (5-char SQLSTATE) and message prefix `'PGAME11: '`. Test signature: anon rpc call returns `{ error: { code: 'PG011', message: /PGAME11/ } }`. App-side handler matches on `error.code === 'PG011'`.
- **T-4 connected-players snapshot pattern (per US-046 / mark_correct_guess):** when an Insider RPC must capture "currently-connected players" into a uuid[] (e.g. `eligible_voter_ids` at vote start), do it inline inside the SAME UPDATE that flips the phase, via a sub-select: `update game_insider_round set …, eligible_voter_ids = (select array_agg(player_id) from players where room_id = p_room_id and connected = true) where …`. This keeps the snapshot atomic with the phase flip — there is no read-then-write window where a player could connect/disconnect between the SELECT and the UPDATE. Disconnected players are EXCLUDED from voting eligibility but voting still proceeds without waiting on them (T-4: 60s window, vote-completion fires when all eligible voters voted, not when all players voted). The `connected bool` column on `players` is the canonical presence signal — it is set by Realtime presence handlers in client code, NOT something Postgres maintains. Reusable for any future "snapshot connected players" need (e.g. score event eligibility, mid-round sync).
- **Master-only RPC pattern with role lookup (per US-045/US-046 / C2.A):** master-only Insider RPCs (`master_respond`, `mark_correct_guess`, future `master_*` RPCs) use a single combined SELECT to fetch BOTH `phase` from `game_insider_round` AND `role` from `game_insider_roles` via a left join keyed on `(room_id, round_number, player_id)`. The left join is critical: a stranger (no role row) yields `v_role IS NULL`, which the `if v_role is null or v_role <> 'master'` guard catches before any phase logic runs. This avoids a separate "is the caller in the room" check (PGAME11) for master-only RPCs — non-room callers fail the role check first with PGAME15. Three-tier guard order after reconcile: (1) role check → PGAME15, (2) `phase = 'result_failed'` → PGAME02 (post-reconcile expiry path), (3) `phase <> 'asking'` → PGAME16. Reusable for any master-only RPC running during the asking phase.
- **TS wrapper convention for game RPCs (per US-050 / FR-2.1, C1.B):** game-specific RPC wrappers live in `apps/<game>/lib/<game>-rpc.ts`, NOT in `packages/core` (per design doc D3 — packages/core is cross-game only). Each wrapper: (1) takes a default-typed `SupabaseClient` as the first arg (matches the packages/core convention so the wrapper isn't coupled to a specific Database type), (2) takes a single camelCase args object as the second arg (`{ roomId, round, playerId, ... }`) so app code reads naturally, (3) calls `dispatch<Record<string, unknown>, TResult>(supabase, "<rpc_name>", { p_room_id: args.roomId, ... })` — translating camelCase → snake_case `p_*` is the wrapper's only real job, (4) returns the unwrapped scalar (or `void`). The `Record<string, unknown>` input type is the contract `dispatch` expects after its internal cast, so making it explicit at the call site keeps inference clean. Errors come back as `GameRpcError` "for free" because every wrapper goes through `dispatch`. Reusable for any future Headball/Hub/Insider RPC wrappers. Test convention: mock `supabase.rpc` with vi.fn, assert the rpcName + snake_case args in one test per wrapper, plus a paired error-propagation test that asserts the rejection is `GameRpcError`.
- **`reconcile_round_phase` self-healing helper (per US-041 / T-2.A):** every Insider RPC begins with `perform reconcile_round_phase(p_room_id, p_round)` so a missed `expire_round` (network blip, tab closed, race) is auto-fixed before the RPC inspects the phase. The helper is a SECURITY DEFINER function with two UPDATE statements: (1) `asking → result_failed` when `now() >= started_at + make_interval(secs => time_limit_s)`, (2) `voting → reveal` when `now() >= vote_deadline`. Other phases and not-yet-expired rounds are no-ops via WHERE-clause filtering — there is no IF/branching, just two filtered UPDATEs. This makes it idempotent (second call is also a no-op) and silent on missing rounds (UPDATE matches zero rows, no error). Use `make_interval(secs => time_limit_s)` rather than `interval '1 second' * time_limit_s` — the latter requires a cast and is less idiomatic. GRANT EXECUTE to anon is required because anon is the only role apps connect with; SECURITY DEFINER lets the helper UPDATE rows even though anon has no direct UPDATE on `game_insider_round`. Always `set search_path = public` on SECURITY DEFINER functions to defend against search_path attacks.

With these workspace deps now declared explicitly, future `bun install` runs in CI / fresh checkout, and the per-app `package.json` accurately reflects what apps/insider needs.

## 2026-05-09 — US-047 (Phase 5a.10 — expire_round RPC, idempotent A3.B)
- TDD red: wrote `apps/insider/lib/__tests__/migration-0026-expire-round.test.ts` (6 tests: (a) 'asking' round 10 minutes past a 5-minute deadline → phase='result_failed' AND RPC returns 1; (b) 'asking' round just-started inside 300s window → no-op + returns 0; (c) phase='preparing' → no-op + returns 0; (d) phase='voting' even with expired vote_deadline → no-op + returns 0 (expire_round is asking-only by contract); (e) two concurrent calls via Promise.all on an expired asking round → sum of update counts = 1 + final phase='result_failed'; (f) missing round (round 999, no row) → silent no-op + returns 0). Ran against schema with no migration 0026 — all 6 failed with `PGRST202 Could not find the function public.expire_round`, the expected red.
- TDD green: wrote `supabase/migrations/0026_expire_round.sql`:
  - `expire_round(p_room_id uuid, p_round int) returns int`. SECURITY DEFINER, `set search_path = public`.
  - Single UPDATE: `update game_insider_round set phase='result_failed' where room_id=p_room_id and round_number=p_round and phase='asking' and started_at is not null and now() >= started_at + make_interval(secs => time_limit_s)`.
  - `get diagnostics v_count = row_count; return v_count;` — exposes rows-affected so tests (and clients, if useful) can detect "I won the race".
  - GRANT EXECUTE to anon. No `perform reconcile_round_phase(...)` here because the RPC IS effectively the asking-half of reconcile — adding the chain would be redundant (identical UPDATE filter) and would mask the row-count return. The voting-half stays inside reconcile_round_phase, which other RPCs continue to chain through.
- Ran `bunx supabase db reset`: all 26 migrations applied clean.
- 6/6 new tests pass; full workspace `bunx vitest run` reports 161/161 across 27 files (was 155/155 — added 6 new tests for this story).
- Regenerated `packages/types/src/database.types.ts` via `bunx supabase gen types typescript --local 2>/dev/null > …`. Verified `expire_round` appears with `Args: { p_room_id: string; p_round: number }, Returns: number`. Per-app `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean across apps/insider, apps/headball, apps/hub.
- `bun run lint` passes (FULL TURBO cache hit on the 2 lint tasks; realtime-publication script green — migration 0026 is function-only, no new tables).
- Files changed: `supabase/migrations/0026_expire_round.sql` (new), `apps/insider/lib/__tests__/migration-0026-expire-round.test.ts` (new), `packages/types/src/database.types.ts` (regen).
- **Learnings:**
  - **Public-facing reconcile slice + helper coexistence:** captured at top of progress.txt. Keeping `expire_round` as a thin asking-only RPC (rather than aliasing it to `reconcile_round_phase` or merging both) preserves a clean boundary: clients fire `expire_round` on a timer event; internal RPCs chain through `reconcile_round_phase`. Both have the same `asking → result_failed` filter so the behavior is identical, but the contracts and call-sites are different.
  - **`returns int` for "rows affected" idempotency contracts:** captured at top of progress.txt. The `Promise.all(... )` test that asserts `a + b === 1` is a much sharper signal than re-reading `phase` (which would always be `result_failed` regardless of who won). When a WHERE-clause guard is the idempotency mechanism, expose the rows-affected so tests can verify exactly-one-mutation directly.
  - **`get diagnostics v_count = row_count;` idiom in plpgsql:** standard PG pattern to capture the count of rows the previous UPDATE/DELETE affected. Must be inside a `declare … begin … end` block (we already declare a local int). Not the same as `FOUND` (boolean: did at least one row match) — `row_count` gives the exact count.
  - **No reconcile chain inside expire_round:** unlike `master_respond`/`mark_correct_guess`/`advance_to_asking`, this RPC does NOT call `perform reconcile_round_phase(...)` first. The reason: its UPDATE filter IS the reconcile filter for the asking half, so chaining would be a no-op (identical predicate) AND would zero out the `row_count` we want to return. The voting half stays inside `reconcile_round_phase` for use by every other Insider RPC. This is the only Insider RPC so far that bypasses the reconcile chain — document it here so future iterations don't "fix" it.

## 2026-05-09 — US-046 (Phase 5a.9 — mark_correct_guess RPC)
- TDD red: wrote `apps/insider/lib/__tests__/migration-0025-mark-correct-guess.test.ts` (5 tests: happy-path Master in 'asking' before deadline → phase='guessed' with vote_deadline≈now()+60s, guessed_at/guessed_by_player_id stamped, eligible_voter_ids snapshot of 4 connected players excluding the disconnected 5th (T-4); non-Master callers (insider/player roles) → PGAME15/PG015 + phase unchanged; phase='preparing' → PGAME16/PG016; phase='guessed' (already-marked) → PGAME16/PG016; round expired (started 60s ago, time_limit=1s) → PGAME02/PG002 + reconcile flips to 'result_failed' + vote_deadline/eligible_voter_ids remain NULL). Ran against schema with no migration 0025 — all 5 failed with `PGRST202 Could not find the function public.mark_correct_guess`, the expected red.
- TDD green: wrote `supabase/migrations/0025_mark_correct_guess.sql`:
  - `mark_correct_guess(p_room_id uuid, p_round int, p_player_id uuid) returns void`. SECURITY DEFINER, `set search_path = public`.
  - Step 1: `perform reconcile_round_phase(p_room_id, p_round)` — self-heal expired deadline first (T-2.A).
  - Step 2: combined `select r.phase, ro.role into v_phase, v_role from game_insider_round r left join game_insider_roles ro on …` — single round-trip fetches both phase and caller's role; left join ensures a stranger surfaces `v_role IS NULL`.
  - Step 3: master-only authorization → `if v_role is null or v_role <> 'master' then raise PGAME15 using errcode = 'PG015'` (covers no-role-row + wrong-role).
  - Step 4: `if v_phase = 'result_failed' then raise PGAME02 using errcode = 'PG002'` — post-reconcile expiry path, distinct from generic phase mismatch.
  - Step 5: `if v_phase <> 'asking' then raise PGAME16 using errcode = 'PG016'` — generic phase guard for preparing/guessed/voting/reveal.
  - Step 6: single UPDATE flips phase='guessed' AND stamps guessed_at=now(), guessed_by_player_id=p_player_id, vote_deadline=now()+interval '60 seconds', eligible_voter_ids = sub-select `array_agg(player_id) from players where room_id = p_room_id and connected = true` (T-4). The WHERE-clause `phase = 'asking'` keeps the UPDATE idempotent if a concurrent call slipped through reconcile.
  - GRANT EXECUTE on `mark_correct_guess(uuid, int, uuid)` to anon.
- Ran `bunx supabase db reset`: all 25 migrations applied clean.
- 5/5 new tests pass; full workspace `bunx vitest run` reports 155/155 across 26 files (was 145/145 — added 5 new tests, plus 5 net-new tests committed in interim work).
- Regenerated `packages/types/src/database.types.ts` via `bunx supabase gen types typescript --local 2>/dev/null > …`. Verified `mark_correct_guess` appears with `Args: { p_player_id: string; p_room_id: string; p_round: number }, Returns: undefined`. `bunx tsc --noEmit` clean across apps/insider, apps/headball, apps/hub.
- `bun run lint` passes (FULL TURBO cache hit on the 2 lint tasks; realtime-publication script green — migration 0025 is function-only, no new tables).
- Updated `packages/core/error-codes.md`: PGAME15/PGAME16 bullets now note that `0025_mark_correct_guess.sql` shares the bindings with `0024_master_respond.sql` (broadened wording from "respond" → "perform this action" / "master-only RPCs that run during the asking phase"). Reserved-codes line updated to drop `mark_correct_guess` from the unassigned-RPC list.
- Files changed: `supabase/migrations/0025_mark_correct_guess.sql` (new), `apps/insider/lib/__tests__/migration-0025-mark-correct-guess.test.ts` (new), `packages/types/src/database.types.ts` (regen), `packages/core/error-codes.md` (broadened PGAME15/16 bindings).
- **Learnings:**
  - **T-4 connected-players snapshot pattern:** captured at top of progress.txt. Inline sub-select inside the UPDATE is the atomic, race-free way to capture "currently-connected players" into a uuid[] snapshot column. Reusable for any future "snapshot at phase entry" need (vote eligibility, score event participants, etc.).
  - **Master-only RPC pattern with role lookup:** captured at top of progress.txt. The combined `phase + role` SELECT via left join replaces the standard "membership check + phase check" two-step for master-only RPCs. Cuts a query, makes the role-fail path PGAME15 instead of PGAME11, and leaves the phase logic untouched for whichever phase the RPC operates in.
  - **PGAME15/PGAME16 are shared bindings, not per-RPC:** master_respond and mark_correct_guess both use PGAME15 (only-master) and PGAME16 (phase-not-asking) because they share authorization shape and operating phase. The error-codes.md bullets now reflect this — when a third master-only-during-asking RPC lands, it should reuse these codes too rather than picking new ones.
  - **`vote_deadline` and `eligible_voter_ids` are populated AT GUESS, not at voting entry:** the design doc state machine (line 542-546) shows snapshot-at-voting-entry, but the PRD acceptance criteria specify snapshot-at-guess. Per the PRD, `mark_correct_guess` writes BOTH the phase flip AND the voting-state setup in one shot, so the guessed→voting auto-advance (driven by 1-2s celebration UI) is purely a phase update with no extra inputs needed. Aligns the data with the moment of decision (master clicks "guessed correctly") rather than the moment of UI transition.
  - **Sub-select column naming inside UPDATE works without aliasing:** `eligible_voter_ids = (select array_agg(player_id) from players where room_id = p_room_id and connected = true)` — `room_id` references the OUTER `players` row, but PostgreSQL resolves it correctly because the UPDATE target is `game_insider_round` (no `room_id` ambiguity at this scope). If the UPDATE were on a `players` table, the sub-select would need explicit aliasing.
